        Project directory:

        Idea:
Replace @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor entirely with a custom lightweight timeline renderer. The library bundles interact.js (~85KB minified) and we only use it for: time ruler rendering, row layout with virtualization, resize via interact.js, and cursor/playhead rendering. We've already reimplemented drag (useClipDrag), drop (useExternalDrop, useDragCoordinator), and cross-track moves ourselves. The goal is to eliminate the ~250KB minified dependency entirely and build a custom <TimelineCanvas> using our existing hooks + a simple pointer-event resize handler. Key files: src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineEditor.tsx (main consumer), useClipDrag.ts, useClipResize.ts, useDragCoordinator.ts, useExternalDrop.ts, coordinate-utils.ts. The library provides: Timeline component from @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor, TimelineAction/TimelineRow/TimelineState types from @xzdarcy/timeline-engine, CSS from @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor/dist/react-timeline-editor.css.

User notes and answers:
- User answers: Q1: Clean reimplementation acceptable, not pixel-perfect parity. Q2: Might have 50+ tracks, so consider virtualization. Q3: Simple pointer-event resize with min-width clamping is sufficient, no inertia/snap needed. Q4: One PR, all in one go.

  "meta_commentary": "EXECUTION GUIDANCE:\n\n**The two hardest parts of this plan are T3 (grid shell) and T4 (resize handles). Get these right and everything else follows.**\n\n**Resize transient state (most likely source of bugs):** The library previously owned action geometry and updated it internally during resize drags. Our useClipResize hook only records start position on resize-start and commits the final edit on resize-end \u2014 it does NOT drive visual updates during the drag. TimelineCanvas must maintain a `resizingOverrides` map (actionId \u2192 {start, end}) that overrides prop-driven positions while a resize is active. During pointermove, update this map AND re-render the affected action at its new width. On pointerup, clear the override and let the committed data flow back through props. If you skip this, clips will appear frozen during resize and then jump to their final position.\n\n**Scroll sync (second most likely regression):** The current layout has track labels in a fixed-width (144px) left sidebar and the Timeline in a flex-1 right pane. These are sibling containers. The library's internal scroll handled vertical alignment implicitly because track labels were outside its scope \u2014 BUT the track-label container has its own vertical overflow. You need to wire an onScroll handler that keeps the track-label pane's scrollTop in sync with the grid's scrollTop (or vice versa). Test this with enough tracks to require scrolling.\n\n**onChange removal safety:** Verify by reading useClipEditing.ts carefully. The onChange handler calls `applyTimelineEdit(nextRows, buildRowTrackPatches(nextRows))`. During library-managed resize, this was the path that updated row data. With custom resize, useClipResize.onActionResizeEnd calls its own applyTimelineEdit. Double-check there's no other code path that triggers onChange.\n\n**DOM query migration (T5) must be done before or simultaneously with T6.** If you swap in TimelineCanvas while drop-position.ts still queries `.timeline-editor-edit-area`, all drops will silently fail or miscalculate. Consider adding the `.timeline-canvas-edit-area` class to the grid container in T3 so T5 can be validated independently.\n\n**pixelsPerSecond calculation:** In the library, `pixelsPerSecond = scaleWidth / scale`. Verify this formula is correct by checking coordinate-utils or the library source. All action positioning depends on this being right.\n\n**Testing approach for T9:** Don't just check that things work \u2014 check that positions are CORRECT. Create a clip, note its from/to times, verify the clip's pixel position matches `time * pixelsPerSecond + startLeft`. Resize a clip and verify the metadata update matches the visual. This catches off-by-one-startLeft errors.\n\n**Single PR:** User wants one PR, all in one go. But develop incrementally \u2014 you can render TimelineCanvas alongside Timeline temporarily during T2-T3 for visual comparison before the swap in T6."

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app

        Idea:
Replace @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor entirely with a custom lightweight timeline renderer. The library bundles interact.js (~85KB minified) and we only use it for: time ruler rendering, row layout with virtualization, resize via interact.js, and cursor/playhead rendering. We've already reimplemented drag (useClipDrag), drop (useExternalDrop, useDragCoordinator), and cross-track moves ourselves. The goal is to eliminate the ~250KB minified dependency entirely and build a custom <TimelineCanvas> using our existing hooks + a simple pointer-event resize handler. Key files: src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineEditor.tsx (main consumer), useClipDrag.ts, useClipResize.ts, useDragCoordinator.ts, useExternalDrop.ts, coordinate-utils.ts. The library provides: Timeline component from @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor, TimelineAction/TimelineRow/TimelineState types from @xzdarcy/timeline-engine, CSS from @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor/dist/react-timeline-editor.css.

User notes and answers:
- User answers: Q1: Clean reimplementation acceptable, not pixel-perfect parity. Q2: Might have 50+ tracks, so consider virtualization. Q3: Simple pointer-event resize with min-width clamping is sufficient, no inertia/snap needed. Q4: One PR, all in one go.

  "meta_commentary": "EXECUTION GUIDANCE:\n\n**The two hardest parts of this plan are T3 (grid shell) and T4 (resize handles). Get these right and everything else follows.**\n\n**Resize transient state (most likely source of bugs):** The library previously owned action geometry and updated it internally during resize drags. Our useClipResize hook only records start position on resize-start and commits the final edit on resize-end \u2014 it does NOT drive visual updates during the drag. TimelineCanvas must maintain a `resizingOverrides` map (actionId \u2192 {start, end}) that overrides prop-driven positions while a resize is active. During pointermove, update this map AND re-render the affected action at its new width. On pointerup, clear the override and let the committed data flow back through props. If you skip this, clips will appear frozen during resize and then jump to their final position.\n\n**Scroll sync (second most likely regression):** The current layout has track labels in a fixed-width (144px) left sidebar and the Timeline in a flex-1 right pane. These are sibling containers. The library's internal scroll handled vertical alignment implicitly because track labels were outside its scope \u2014 BUT the track-label container has its own vertical overflow. You need to wire an onScroll handler that keeps the track-label pane's scrollTop in sync with the grid's scrollTop (or vice versa). Test this with enough tracks to require scrolling.\n\n**onChange removal safety:** Verify by reading useClipEditing.ts carefully. The onChange handler calls `applyTimelineEdit(nextRows, buildRowTrackPatches(nextRows))`. During library-managed resize, this was the path that updated row data. With custom resize, useClipResize.onActionResizeEnd calls its own applyTimelineEdit. Double-check there's no other code path that triggers onChange.\n\n**DOM query migration (T5) must be done before or simultaneously with T6.** If you swap in TimelineCanvas while drop-position.ts still queries `.timeline-editor-edit-area`, all drops will silently fail or miscalculate. Consider adding the `.timeline-canvas-edit-area` class to the grid container in T3 so T5 can be validated independently.\n\n**pixelsPerSecond calculation:** In the library, `pixelsPerSecond = scaleWidth / scale`. Verify this formula is correct by checking coordinate-utils or the library source. All action positioning depends on this being right.\n\n**Testing approach for T9:** Don't just check that things work \u2014 check that positions are CORRECT. Create a clip, note its from/to times, verify the clip's pixel position matches `time * pixelsPerSecond + startLeft`. Resize a clip and verify the metadata update matches the visual. This catches off-by-one-startLeft errors.\n\n**Single PR:** User wants one PR, all in one go. But develop incrementally \u2014 you can render TimelineCanvas alongside Timeline temporarily during T2-T3 for visual comparison before the swap in T6."
}

{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimeRuler.tsx\n+import { useEffect, useMemo, useRef, useState } from 'react';\n+import { formatTime } from '@/tools/video-editor/lib/coordinate-utils';\n+\n+const POINTER_DRAG_THRESHOLD_PX = 3;\n+const OVERSCAN_STEPS = 4;\n+\n+export interface TimeRulerProps {\n+  scale: number;\n+  scaleWidth: number;\n+  scaleSplitCount: number;\n+  startLeft: number;\n+  scrollLeft: number;\n+  totalWidth: number;\n+  onClickTimeArea: (time: number) => void;\n+  onCursorDrag: (time: number) => void;\n+}\n+\n+interface PointerSession {\n+  pointerId: number;\n+  startClientX: number;\n+  isDragging: boolean;\n+}\n+\n+const clamp = (value: number, min: number, max: number): number => Math.min(max, Math.max(min, value));\n+\n+export function TimeRuler({\n+  scale,\n+  scaleWidth,\n+  scaleSplitCount,\n+  startLeft,\n+  scrollLeft,\n+  totalWidth,\n+  onClickTimeArea,\n+  onCursorDrag,\n+}: TimeRulerProps) {\n+  const containerRef = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null);\n+  const pointerSessionRef = useRef<PointerSession | null>(null);\n+  const [viewportWidth, setViewportWidth] = useState(0);\n+\n+  const safeSplitCount = Math.max(1, scaleSplitCount);\n+  const contentWidth = Math.max(totalWidth, startLeft + scaleWidth);\n+  const pixelsPerSecond = scaleWidth / Math.max(scale, Number.EPSILON);\n+  const minorStepWidth = scaleWidth / safeSplitCount;\n+  const maxTime = Math.max(0, (contentWidth - startLeft) / pixelsPerSecond);\n+\n+  useEffect(() => {\n+    const element = containerRef.current;\n+    if (!element) {\n+      return undefined;\n+    }\n+\n+    const updateWidth = () => setViewportWidth(element.clientWidth);\n+    updateWidth();\n+\n+    const observer = new ResizeObserver(updateWidth);\n+    observer.observe(element);\n+    return () => observer.disconnect();\n+  }, []);\n+\n+  const visibleRange = useMemo(() => {\n+    const viewWidth = viewportWidth || contentWidth;\n+    const visibleLeft = Math.max(0, scrollLeft);\n+    const visibleRight = Math.min(contentWidth, visibleLeft + viewWidth);\n+    const firstMinorIndex = Math.max(\n+      0,\n+      Math.floor((visibleLeft - startLeft) / minorStepWidth) - OVERSCAN_STEPS,\n+    );\n+    const lastMinorIndex = Math.max(\n+      firstMinorIndex,\n+      Math.ceil((visibleRight - startLeft) / minorStepWidth) + OVERSCAN_STEPS,\n+    );\n+\n+    return { firstMinorIndex, lastMinorIndex };\n+  }, [contentWidth, minorStepWidth, scrollLeft, startLeft, viewportWidth]);\n+\n+  const majorTicks = useMemo(() => {\n+    const firstMajorIndex = Math.floor(visibleRange.firstMinorIndex / safeSplitCount);\n+    const lastMajorIndex = Math.ceil(visibleRange.lastMinorIndex / safeSplitCount);\n+    const ticks: Array<{ key: string; left: number; label: string }> = [];\n+\n+    for (let index = firstMajorIndex; index <= lastMajorIndex; index += 1) {\n+      const left = startLeft + index * scaleWidth;\n+      if (left > contentWidth) {\n+        break;\n+      }\n+\n+      ticks.push({\n+        key: `major-${index}`,\n+        left,\n+        label: formatTime(index * scale),\n+      });\n+    }\n+\n+    return ticks;\n+  }, [contentWidth, safeSplitCount, scale, scaleWidth, startLeft, visibleRange.firstMinorIndex, visibleRange.lastMinorIndex]);\n+\n+  const minorTicks = useMemo(() => {\n+    const ticks: Array<{ key: string; left: number; isMajor: boolean }> = [];\n+\n+    for (let index = visibleRange.firstMinorIndex; index <= visibleRange.lastMinorIndex; index += 1) {\n+      const left = startLeft + index * minorStepWidth;\n+      if (left < startLeft || left > contentWidth) {\n+        continue;\n+      }\n+\n+      ticks.push({\n+        key: `minor-${index}`,\n+        left,\n+        isMajor: index % safeSplitCount === 0,\n+      });\n+    }\n+\n+    return ticks;\n+  }, [contentWidth, minorStepWidth, safeSplitCount, startLeft, visibleRange.firstMinorIndex, visibleRange.lastMinorIndex]);\n+\n+  const clientXToTime = (clientX: number): number => {\n+    const rect = containerRef.current?.getBoundingClientRect();\n+    if (!rect) {\n+      return 0;\n+    }\n+\n+    const relativeX = clientX - rect.left + scrollLeft;\n+    return clamp((relativeX - startLeft) / pixelsPerSecond, 0, maxTime);\n+  };\n+\n+  const handlePointerDown = (event: React.PointerEvent<HTMLDivElement>) => {\n+    if (event.button !== 0) {\n+      return;\n+    }\n+\n+    pointerSessionRef.current = {\n+      pointerId: event.pointerId,\n+      startClientX: event.clientX,\n+      isDragging: false,\n+    };\n+    event.currentTarget.setPointerCapture(event.pointerId);\n+    event.preventDefault();\n+  };\n+\n+  const handlePointerMove = (event: React.PointerEvent<HTMLDivElement>) => {\n+    const session = pointerSessionRef.current;\n+    if (!session || session.pointerId !== event.pointerId) {\n+      return;\n+    }\n+\n+    if (!session.isDragging && Math.abs(event.clientX - session.startClientX) >= POINTER_DRAG_THRESHOLD_PX) {\n+      session.isDragging = true;\n+    }\n+\n+    if (!session.isDragging) {\n+      return;\n+    }\n+\n+    onCursorDrag(clientXToTime(event.clientX));\n+  };\n+\n+  const finishPointerSession = (\n+    event: React.PointerEvent<HTMLDivElement>,\n+    cancelled: boolean,\n+  ) => {\n+    const session = pointerSessionRef.current;\n+    if (!session || session.pointerId !== event.pointerId) {\n+      return;\n+    }\n+\n+    pointerSessionRef.current = null;\n+    if (event.currentTarget.hasPointerCapture(event.pointerId)) {\n+      event.currentTarget.releasePointerCapture(event.pointerId);\n+    }\n+\n+    if (cancelled) {\n+      return;\n+    }\n+\n+    const time = clientXToTime(event.clientX);\n+    if (session.isDragging) {\n+      onCursorDrag(time);\n+      return;\n+    }\n+\n+    onClickTimeArea(time);\n+  };\n+\n+  return (\n+    <div\n+      ref={containerRef}\n+      className=\"relative h-[30px] overflow-hidden border-b border-border bg-zinc-950/80 select-none\"\n+    >\n+      <div\n+        className=\"absolute inset-y-0 left-0\"\n+        style={{\n+          width: contentWidth,\n+          transform: `translateX(${-scrollLeft}px)`,\n+        }}\n+      >\n+        <div\n+          className=\"absolute inset-y-0 left-0 border-r border-border/70 bg-zinc-950/95\"\n+          style={{ width: startLeft }}\n+        />\n+\n+        {minorTicks.map((tick) => (\n+          <div\n+            key={tick.key}\n+            className={tick.isMajor ? 'absolute bottom-0 w-px bg-border/80' : 'absolute bottom-0 w-px bg-border/35'}\n+            style={{\n+              left: tick.left,\n+              height: tick.isMajor ? '100%' : '45%',\n+            }}\n+          />\n+        ))}\n+\n+        {majorTicks.map((tick) => (\n+          <div\n+            key={tick.key}\n+            className=\"pointer-events-none absolute left-0 top-0\"\n+            style={{\n+              transform: `translateX(${tick.left + 6}px)`,\n+            }}\n+          >\n+            <span className=\"rounded-sm bg-zinc-950/85 px-1 py-0.5 font-mono text-[10px] tracking-[0.08em] text-zinc-400\">\n+              {tick.label}\n+            </span>\n+          </div>\n+        ))}\n+      </div>\n+\n+      <div\n+        className=\"absolute inset-0 cursor-pointer touch-none\"\n+        onPointerDown={handlePointerDown}\n+        onPointerMove={handlePointerMove}\n+        onPointerUp={(event) => finishPointerSession(event, false)}\n+        onPointerCancel={(event) => finishPointerSession(event, true)}\n+      />\n+    </div>\n+  );\n+}\n*** End Patch\n"
}

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app

        Idea:
Replace @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor entirely with a custom lightweight timeline renderer. The library bundles interact.js (~85KB minified) and we only use it for: time ruler rendering, row layout with virtualization, resize via interact.js, and cursor/playhead rendering. We've already reimplemented drag (useClipDrag), drop (useExternalDrop, useDragCoordinator), and cross-track moves ourselves. The goal is to eliminate the ~250KB minified dependency entirely and build a custom <TimelineCanvas> using our existing hooks + a simple pointer-event resize handler. Key files: src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineEditor.tsx (main consumer), useClipDrag.ts, useClipResize.ts, useDragCoordinator.ts, useExternalDrop.ts, coordinate-utils.ts. The library provides: Timeline component from @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor, TimelineAction/TimelineRow/TimelineState types from @xzdarcy/timeline-engine, CSS from @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor/dist/react-timeline-editor.css.

User notes and answers:
- User answers: Q1: Clean reimplementation acceptable, not pixel-perfect parity. Q2: Might have 50+ tracks, so consider virtualization. Q3: Simple pointer-event resize with min-width clamping is sufficient, no inertia/snap needed. Q4: One PR, all in one go.

      "id": "T4",
      "description": "Add pointer-event resize handles to each action wrapper in `<TimelineCanvas>`. Left and right thin (8px) absolute-positioned divs with `cursor: ew-resize`. On pointerdown: capture pointer, record initial start/end and clientX, fire `onActionResizeStart({action, row, dir})`. On pointermove: compute new start or end from delta, clamp >= 0 and enforce minimum width, update TRANSIENT local action geometry in TimelineCanvas state so the clip visually resizes during drag, and fire `onActionResizing({action, row, dir})`. On pointerup: fire `onActionResizeEnd({action, row, dir})` and clear transient state. CRITICAL: TimelineCanvas must own a transient override map (e.g. `resizingOverrides: Map<actionId, {start, end}>`) that takes precedence over prop-driven positions during active resize. Without this, clips will snap rather than smoothly resize. The callback signatures must match what `useClipResize.ts` expects so it requires zero changes.",

  "meta_commentary": "EXECUTION GUIDANCE:\n\n**The two hardest parts of this plan are T3 (grid shell) and T4 (resize handles). Get these right and everything else follows.**\n\n**Resize transient state (most likely source of bugs):** The library previously owned action geometry and updated it internally during resize drags. Our useClipResize hook only records start position on resize-start and commits the final edit on resize-end \u2014 it does NOT drive visual updates during the drag. TimelineCanvas must maintain a `resizingOverrides` map (actionId \u2192 {start, end}) that overrides prop-driven positions while a resize is active. During pointermove, update this map AND re-render the affected action at its new width. On pointerup, clear the override and let the committed data flow back through props. If you skip this, clips will appear frozen during resize and then jump to their final position.\n\n**Scroll sync (second most likely regression):** The current layout has track labels in a fixed-width (144px) left sidebar and the Timeline in a flex-1 right pane. These are sibling containers. The library's internal scroll handled vertical alignment implicitly because track labels were outside its scope \u2014 BUT the track-label container has its own vertical overflow. You need to wire an onScroll handler that keeps the track-label pane's scrollTop in sync with the grid's scrollTop (or vice versa). Test this with enough tracks to require scrolling.\n\n**onChange removal safety:** Verify by reading useClipEditing.ts carefully. The onChange handler calls `applyTimelineEdit(nextRows, buildRowTrackPatches(nextRows))`. During library-managed resize, this was the path that updated row data. With custom resize, useClipResize.onActionResizeEnd calls its own applyTimelineEdit. Double-check there's no other code path that triggers onChange.\n\n**DOM query migration (T5) must be done before or simultaneously with T6.** If you swap in TimelineCanvas while drop-position.ts still queries `.timeline-editor-edit-area`, all drops will silently fail or miscalculate. Consider adding the `.timeline-canvas-edit-area` class to the grid container in T3 so T5 can be validated independently.\n\n**pixelsPerSecond calculation:** In the library, `pixelsPerSecond = scaleWidth / scale`. Verify this formula is correct by checking coordinate-utils or the library source. All action positioning depends on this being right.\n\n**Testing approach for T9:** Don't just check that things work \u2014 check that positions are CORRECT. Create a clip, note its from/to times, verify the clip's pixel position matches `time * pixelsPerSecond + startLeft`. Resize a clip and verify the metadata update matches the visual. This catches off-by-one-startLeft errors.\n\n**Single PR:** User wants one PR, all in one go. But develop incrementally \u2014 you can render TimelineCanvas alongside Timeline temporarily during T2-T3 for visual comparison before the swap in T6."

        Project directory:

        Idea:
Replace @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor entirely with a custom lightweight timeline renderer. The library bundles interact.js (~85KB minified) and we only use it for: time ruler rendering, row layout with virtualization, resize via interact.js, and cursor/playhead rendering. We've already reimplemented drag (useClipDrag), drop (useExternalDrop, useDragCoordinator), and cross-track moves ourselves. The goal is to eliminate the ~250KB minified dependency entirely and build a custom <TimelineCanvas> using our existing hooks + a simple pointer-event resize handler. Key files: src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineEditor.tsx (main consumer), useClipDrag.ts, useClipResize.ts, useDragCoordinator.ts, useExternalDrop.ts, coordinate-utils.ts. The library provides: Timeline component from @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor, TimelineAction/TimelineRow/TimelineState types from @xzdarcy/timeline-engine, CSS from @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor/dist/react-timeline-editor.css.

User notes and answers:
- User answers: Q1: Clean reimplementation acceptable, not pixel-perfect parity. Q2: Might have 50+ tracks, so consider virtualization. Q3: Simple pointer-event resize with min-width clamping is sufficient, no inertia/snap needed. Q4: One PR, all in one go.

  {
    "id": "T4",
    "description": "Add pointer-event resize handles to each action wrapper in `<TimelineCanvas>`. Left and right thin (8px) absolute-positioned divs with `cursor: ew-resize`. On pointerdown: capture pointer, record initial start/end and clientX, fire `onActionResizeStart({action, row, dir})`. On pointermove: compute new start or end from delta, clamp >= 0 and enforce minimum width, update TRANSIENT local action geometry in TimelineCanvas state so the clip visually resizes during drag, and fire `onActionResizing({action, row, dir})`. On pointerup: fire `onActionResizeEnd({action, row, dir})` and clear transient state. CRITICAL: TimelineCanvas must own a transient override map (e.g. `resizingOverrides: Map<actionId, {start, end}>`) that takes precedence over prop-driven positions during active resize. Without this, clips will snap rather than smoothly resize. The callback signatures must match what `useClipResize.ts` expects so it requires zero changes.",

      "T3"
    ],
    "status": "pending",

  "meta_commentary": "EXECUTION GUIDANCE:\n\n**The two hardest parts of this plan are T3 (grid shell) and T4 (resize handles). Get these right and everything else follows.**\n\n**Resize transient state (most likely source of bugs):** The library previously owned action geometry and updated it internally during resize drags. Our useClipResize hook only records start position on resize-start and commits the final edit on resize-end \u2014 it does NOT drive visual updates during the drag. TimelineCanvas must maintain a `resizingOverrides` map (actionId \u2192 {start, end}) that overrides prop-driven positions while a resize is active. During pointermove, update this map AND re-render the affected action at its new width. On pointerup, clear the override and let the committed data flow back through props. If you skip this, clips will appear frozen during resize and then jump to their final position.\n\n**Scroll sync (second most likely regression):** The current layout has track labels in a fixed-width (144px) left sidebar and the Timeline in a flex-1 right pane. These are sibling containers. The library's internal scroll handled vertical alignment implicitly because track labels were outside its scope \u2014 BUT the track-label container has its own vertical overflow. You need to wire an onScroll handler that keeps the track-label pane's scrollTop in sync with the grid's scrollTop (or vice versa). Test this with enough tracks to require scrolling.\n\n**onChange removal safety:** Verify by reading useClipEditing.ts carefully. The onChange handler calls `applyTimelineEdit(nextRows, buildRowTrackPatches(nextRows))`. During library-managed resize, this was the path that updated row data. With custom resize, useClipResize.onActionResizeEnd calls its own applyTimelineEdit. Double-check there's no other code path that triggers onChange.\n\n**DOM query migration (T5) must be done before or simultaneously with T6.** If you swap in TimelineCanvas while drop-position.ts still queries `.timeline-editor-edit-area`, all drops will silently fail or miscalculate. Consider adding the `.timeline-canvas-edit-area` class to the grid container in T3 so T5 can be validated independently.\n\n**pixelsPerSecond calculation:** In the library, `pixelsPerSecond = scaleWidth / scale`. Verify this formula is correct by checking coordinate-utils or the library source. All action positioning depends on this being right.\n\n**Testing approach for T9:** Don't just check that things work \u2014 check that positions are CORRECT. Create a clip, note its from/to times, verify the clip's pixel position matches `time * pixelsPerSecond + startLeft`. Resize a clip and verify the metadata update matches the visual. This catches off-by-one-startLeft errors.\n\n**Single PR:** User wants one PR, all in one go. But develop incrementally \u2014 you can render TimelineCanvas alongside Timeline temporarily during T2-T3 for visual comparison before the swap in T6."
}

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app

        Idea:
Replace @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor entirely with a custom lightweight timeline renderer. The library bundles interact.js (~85KB minified) and we only use it for: time ruler rendering, row layout with virtualization, resize via interact.js, and cursor/playhead rendering. We've already reimplemented drag (useClipDrag), drop (useExternalDrop, useDragCoordinator), and cross-track moves ourselves. The goal is to eliminate the ~250KB minified dependency entirely and build a custom <TimelineCanvas> using our existing hooks + a simple pointer-event resize handler. Key files: src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineEditor.tsx (main consumer), useClipDrag.ts, useClipResize.ts, useDragCoordinator.ts, useExternalDrop.ts, coordinate-utils.ts. The library provides: Timeline component from @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor, TimelineAction/TimelineRow/TimelineState types from @xzdarcy/timeline-engine, CSS from @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor/dist/react-timeline-editor.css.

User notes and answers:
- User answers: Q1: Clean reimplementation acceptable, not pixel-perfect parity. Q2: Might have 50+ tracks, so consider virtualization. Q3: Simple pointer-event resize with min-width clamping is sufficient, no inertia/snap needed. Q4: One PR, all in one go.

Accepts `getActionRender(action, row)` for custom clip content. Native `overflow: auto` scrolling. Background grid lines via repeating-linear-gradient.   {
    "id": "T4",
    "description": "Add pointer-event resize handles to each action wrapper in `<TimelineCanvas>`. Left and right thin (8px) absolute-positioned divs with `cursor: ew-resize`. On pointerdown: capture pointer, record initial start/end and clientX, fire `onActionResizeStart({action, row, dir})`. On pointermove: compute new start or end from delta, clamp >= 0 and enforce minimum width, update TRANSIENT local action geometry in TimelineCanvas state so the clip visually resizes during drag, and fire `onActionResizing({action, row, dir})`. On pointerup: fire `onActionResizeEnd({action, row, dir})` and clear transient state. CRITICAL: TimelineCanvas must own a transient override map (e.g. `resizingOverrides: Map<actionId, {start, end}>`) that takes precedence over prop-driven positions during active resize. Without this, clips will snap rather than smoothly resize. The callback signatures must match what `useClipResize.ts` expects so it requires zero changes.",
    "depends_on": [
      "T3"
    ],
    "status": "done",
    "executor_notes": "Updated `src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineCanvas.tsx` with left/right 8px pointer resize handles on each action wrapper. The component now owns transient resize geometry in `resizeOverrides`, mirrored in `resizePreviewRef` so pointerup uses the latest drag position without waiting for React state. Pointerdown captures the handle pointer, fires `onActionResizeStart({ action, row, dir })`, pointermove updates the transient geometry and fires `onActionResizing({ action, row, start, end, dir })`, and pointerup fires `onActionResizeEnd({ action, row, start, end, dir })` before clearing the override. This preserves `useClipResize.ts` callback compatibility without any hook changes. Verification: `npx tsc --noEmit --pretty false` exits 0.",
    "files_changed": [

      "npx tsc --noEmit --pretty false"
    ],

  "meta_commentary": "EXECUTION GUIDANCE:\n\n**The two hardest parts of this plan are T3 (grid shell) and T4 (resize handles). Get these right and everything else follows.**\n\n**Resize transient state (most likely source of bugs):** The library previously owned action geometry and updated it internally during resize drags. Our useClipResize hook only records start position on resize-start and commits the final edit on resize-end \u2014 it does NOT drive visual updates during the drag. TimelineCanvas must maintain a `resizingOverrides` map (actionId \u2192 {start, end}) that overrides prop-driven positions while a resize is active. During pointermove, update this map AND re-render the affected action at its new width. On pointerup, clear the override and let the committed data flow back through props. If you skip this, clips will appear frozen during resize and then jump to their final position.\n\n**Scroll sync (second most likely regression):** The current layout has track labels in a fixed-width (144px) left sidebar and the Timeline in a flex-1 right pane. These are sibling containers. The library's internal scroll handled vertical alignment implicitly because track labels were outside its scope \u2014 BUT the track-label container has its own vertical overflow. You need to wire an onScroll handler that keeps the track-label pane's scrollTop in sync with the grid's scrollTop (or vice versa). Test this with enough tracks to require scrolling.\n\n**onChange removal safety:** Verify by reading useClipEditing.ts carefully. The onChange handler calls `applyTimelineEdit(nextRows, buildRowTrackPatches(nextRows))`. During library-managed resize, this was the path that updated row data. With custom resize, useClipResize.onActionResizeEnd calls its own applyTimelineEdit. Double-check there's no other code path that triggers onChange.\n\n**DOM query migration (T5) must be done before or simultaneously with T6.** If you swap in TimelineCanvas while drop-position.ts still queries `.timeline-editor-edit-area`, all drops will silently fail or miscalculate. Consider adding the `.timeline-canvas-edit-area` class to the grid container in T3 so T5 can be validated independently.\n\n**pixelsPerSecond calculation:** In the library, `pixelsPerSecond = scaleWidth / scale`. Verify this formula is correct by checking coordinate-utils or the library source. All action positioning depends on this being right.\n\n**Testing approach for T9:** Don't just check that things work \u2014 check that positions are CORRECT. Create a clip, note its from/to times, verify the clip's pixel position matches `time * pixelsPerSecond + startLeft`. Resize a clip and verify the metadata update matches the visual. This catches off-by-one-startLeft errors.\n\n**Single PR:** User wants one PR, all in one go. But develop incrementally \u2014 you can render TimelineCanvas alongside Timeline temporarily during T2-T3 for visual comparison before the swap in T6."
}

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app

        Idea:
Replace @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor entirely with a custom lightweight timeline renderer. The library bundles interact.js (~85KB minified) and we only use it for: time ruler rendering, row layout with virtualization, resize via interact.js, and cursor/playhead rendering. We've already reimplemented drag (useClipDrag), drop (useExternalDrop, useDragCoordinator), and cross-track moves ourselves. The goal is to eliminate the ~250KB minified dependency entirely and build a custom <TimelineCanvas> using our existing hooks + a simple pointer-event resize handler. Key files: src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineEditor.tsx (main consumer), useClipDrag.ts, useClipResize.ts, useDragCoordinator.ts, useExternalDrop.ts, coordinate-utils.ts. The library provides: Timeline component from @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor, TimelineAction/TimelineRow/TimelineState types from @xzdarcy/timeline-engine, CSS from @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor/dist/react-timeline-editor.css.

User notes and answers:
- User answers: Q1: Clean reimplementation acceptable, not pixel-perfect parity. Q2: Might have 50+ tracks, so consider virtualization. Q3: Simple pointer-event resize with min-width clamping is sufficient, no inertia/snap needed. Q4: One PR, all in one go.

    "id": "T4",
    "description": "Add pointer-event resize handles to each action wrapper in `<TimelineCanvas>`. Left and right thin (8px) absolute-positioned divs with `cursor: ew-resize`. On pointerdown: capture pointer, record initial start/end and clientX, fire `onActionResizeStart({action, row, dir})`. On pointermove: compute new start or end from delta, clamp >= 0 and enforce minimum width, update TRANSIENT local action geometry in TimelineCanvas state so the clip visually resizes during drag, and fire `onActionResizing({action, row, dir})`. On pointerup: fire `onActionResizeEnd({action, row, dir})` and clear transient state. CRITICAL: TimelineCanvas must own a transient override map (e.g. `resizingOverrides: Map<actionId, {start, end}>`) that takes precedence over prop-driven positions during active resize. Without this, clips will snap rather than smoothly resize. The callback signatures must match what `useClipResize.ts` expects so it requires zero changes.",

  "meta_commentary": "EXECUTION GUIDANCE:\n\n**The two hardest parts of this plan are T3 (grid shell) and T4 (resize handles). Get these right and everything else follows.**\n\n**Resize transient state (most likely source of bugs):** The library previously owned action geometry and updated it internally during resize drags. Our useClipResize hook only records start position on resize-start and commits the final edit on resize-end \u2014 it does NOT drive visual updates during the drag. TimelineCanvas must maintain a `resizingOverrides` map (actionId \u2192 {start, end}) that overrides prop-driven positions while a resize is active. During pointermove, update this map AND re-render the affected action at its new width. On pointerup, clear the override and let the committed data flow back through props. If you skip this, clips will appear frozen during resize and then jump to their final position.\n\n**Scroll sync (second most likely regression):** The current layout has track labels in a fixed-width (144px) left sidebar and the Timeline in a flex-1 right pane. These are sibling containers. The library's internal scroll handled vertical alignment implicitly because track labels were outside its scope \u2014 BUT the track-label container has its own vertical overflow. You need to wire an onScroll handler that keeps the track-label pane's scrollTop in sync with the grid's scrollTop (or vice versa). Test this with enough tracks to require scrolling.\n\n**onChange removal safety:** Verify by reading useClipEditing.ts carefully. The onChange handler calls `applyTimelineEdit(nextRows, buildRowTrackPatches(nextRows))`. During library-managed resize, this was the path that updated row data. With custom resize, useClipResize.onActionResizeEnd calls its own applyTimelineEdit. Double-check there's no other code path that triggers onChange.\n\n**DOM query migration (T5) must be done before or simultaneously with T6.** If you swap in TimelineCanvas while drop-position.ts still queries `.timeline-editor-edit-area`, all drops will silently fail or miscalculate. Consider adding the `.timeline-canvas-edit-area` class to the grid container in T3 so T5 can be validated independently.\n\n**pixelsPerSecond calculation:** In the library, `pixelsPerSecond = scaleWidth / scale`. Verify this formula is correct by checking coordinate-utils or the library source. All action positioning depends on this being right.\n\n**Testing approach for T9:** Don't just check that things work \u2014 check that positions are CORRECT. Create a clip, note its from/to times, verify the clip's pixel position matches `time * pixelsPerSecond + startLeft`. Resize a clip and verify the metadata update matches the visual. This catches off-by-one-startLeft errors.\n\n**Single PR:** User wants one PR, all in one go. But develop incrementally \u2014 you can render TimelineCanvas alongside Timeline temporarily during T2-T3 for visual comparison before the swap in T6."

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app

        Idea:
Replace @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor entirely with a custom lightweight timeline renderer. The library bundles interact.js (~85KB minified) and we only use it for: time ruler rendering, row layout with virtualization, resize via interact.js, and cursor/playhead rendering. We've already reimplemented drag (useClipDrag), drop (useExternalDrop, useDragCoordinator), and cross-track moves ourselves. The goal is to eliminate the ~250KB minified dependency entirely and build a custom <TimelineCanvas> using our existing hooks + a simple pointer-event resize handler. Key files: src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineEditor.tsx (main consumer), useClipDrag.ts, useClipResize.ts, useDragCoordinator.ts, useExternalDrop.ts, coordinate-utils.ts. The library provides: Timeline component from @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor, TimelineAction/TimelineRow/TimelineState types from @xzdarcy/timeline-engine, CSS from @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor/dist/react-timeline-editor.css.

User notes and answers:
- User answers: Q1: Clean reimplementation acceptable, not pixel-perfect parity. Q2: Might have 50+ tracks, so consider virtualization. Q3: Simple pointer-event resize with min-width clamping is sufficient, no inertia/snap needed. Q4: One PR, all in one go.

    "id": "T4",
    "description": "Add pointer-event resize handles to each action wrapper in `<TimelineCanvas>`. Left and right thin (8px) absolute-positioned divs with `cursor: ew-resize`. On pointerdown: capture pointer, record initial start/end and clientX, fire `onActionResizeStart({action, row, dir})`. On pointermove: compute new start or end from delta, clamp >= 0 and enforce minimum width, update TRANSIENT local action geometry in TimelineCanvas state so the clip visually resizes during drag, and fire `onActionResizing({action, row, dir})`. On pointerup: fire `onActionResizeEnd({action, row, dir})` and clear transient state. CRITICAL: TimelineCanvas must own a transient override map (e.g. `resizingOverrides: Map<actionId, {start, end}>`) that takes precedence over prop-driven positions during active resize. Without this, clips will snap rather than smoothly resize. The callback signatures must match what `useClipResize.ts` expects so it requires zero changes.",

Pointerdown captures the handle pointer, fires `onActionResizeStart({ action, row, dir })`, pointermove updates the transient geometry and fires `onActionResizing({ action, row, start, end, dir })`, and pointerup fires `onActionResizeEnd({ action, row, start, end, dir })` before clearing the override.   "meta_commentary": "EXECUTION GUIDANCE:\n\n**The two hardest parts of this plan are T3 (grid shell) and T4 (resize handles). Get these right and everything else follows.**\n\n**Resize transient state (most likely source of bugs):** The library previously owned action geometry and updated it internally during resize drags. Our useClipResize hook only records start position on resize-start and commits the final edit on resize-end \u2014 it does NOT drive visual updates during the drag. TimelineCanvas must maintain a `resizingOverrides` map (actionId \u2192 {start, end}) that overrides prop-driven positions while a resize is active. During pointermove, update this map AND re-render the affected action at its new width. On pointerup, clear the override and let the committed data flow back through props. If you skip this, clips will appear frozen during resize and then jump to their final position.\n\n**Scroll sync (second most likely regression):** The current layout has track labels in a fixed-width (144px) left sidebar and the Timeline in a flex-1 right pane. These are sibling containers. The library's internal scroll handled vertical alignment implicitly because track labels were outside its scope \u2014 BUT the track-label container has its own vertical overflow. You need to wire an onScroll handler that keeps the track-label pane's scrollTop in sync with the grid's scrollTop (or vice versa). Test this with enough tracks to require scrolling.\n\n**onChange removal safety:** Verify by reading useClipEditing.ts carefully. The onChange handler calls `applyTimelineEdit(nextRows, buildRowTrackPatches(nextRows))`. During library-managed resize, this was the path that updated row data. With custom resize, useClipResize.onActionResizeEnd calls its own applyTimelineEdit. Double-check there's no other code path that triggers onChange.\n\n**DOM query migration (T5) must be done before or simultaneously with T6.** If you swap in TimelineCanvas while drop-position.ts still queries `.timeline-editor-edit-area`, all drops will silently fail or miscalculate. Consider adding the `.timeline-canvas-edit-area` class to the grid container in T3 so T5 can be validated independently.\n\n**pixelsPerSecond calculation:** In the library, `pixelsPerSecond = scaleWidth / scale`. Verify this formula is correct by checking coordinate-utils or the library source. All action positioning depends on this being right.\n\n**Testing approach for T9:** Don't just check that things work \u2014 check that positions are CORRECT. Create a clip, note its from/to times, verify the clip's pixel position matches `time * pixelsPerSecond + startLeft`. Resize a clip and verify the metadata update matches the visual. This catches off-by-one-startLeft errors.\n\n**Single PR:** User wants one PR, all in one go. But develop incrementally \u2014 you can render TimelineCanvas alongside Timeline temporarily during T2-T3 for visual comparison before the swap in T6."
}

> DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app

        Idea:
Replace @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor entirely with a custom lightweight timeline renderer. The library bundles interact.js (~85KB minified) and we only use it for: time ruler rendering, row layout with virtualization, resize via interact.js, and cursor/playhead rendering. We've already reimplemented drag (useClipDrag), drop (useExternalDrop, useDragCoordinator), and cross-track moves ourselves. The goal is to eliminate the ~250KB minified dependency entirely and build a custom <TimelineCanvas> using our existing hooks + a simple pointer-event resize handler. Key files: src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineEditor.tsx (main consumer), useClipDrag.ts, useClipResize.ts, useDragCoordinator.ts, useExternalDrop.ts, coordinate-utils.ts. The library provides: Timeline component from @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor, TimelineAction/TimelineRow/TimelineState types from @xzdarcy/timeline-engine, CSS from @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor/dist/react-timeline-editor.css.

User notes and answers:
- User answers: Q1: Clean reimplementation acceptable, not pixel-perfect parity. Q2: Might have 50+ tracks, so consider virtualization. Q3: Simple pointer-event resize with min-width clamping is sufficient, no inertia/snap needed. Q4: One PR, all in one go.

    "description": "Add pointer-event resize handles to each action wrapper in `<TimelineCanvas>`. Left and right thin (8px) absolute-positioned divs with `cursor: ew-resize`. On pointerdown: capture pointer, record initial start/end and clientX, fire `onActionResizeStart({action, row, dir})`. On pointermove: compute new start or end from delta, clamp >= 0 and enforce minimum width, update TRANSIENT local action geometry in TimelineCanvas state so the clip visually resizes during drag, and fire `onActionResizing({action, row, dir})`. On pointerup: fire `onActionResizeEnd({action, row, dir})` and clear transient state. CRITICAL: TimelineCanvas must own a transient override map (e.g. `resizingOverrides: Map<actionId, {start, end}>`) that takes precedence over prop-driven positions during active resize. Without this, clips will snap rather than smoothly resize. The callback signatures must match what `useClipResize.ts` expects so it requires zero changes.",

  "meta_commentary": "EXECUTION GUIDANCE:\n\n**The two hardest parts of this plan are T3 (grid shell) and T4 (resize handles). Get these right and everything else follows.**\n\n**Resize transient state (most likely source of bugs):** The library previously owned action geometry and updated it internally during resize drags. Our useClipResize hook only records start position on resize-start and commits the final edit on resize-end \u2014 it does NOT drive visual updates during the drag. TimelineCanvas must maintain a `resizingOverrides` map (actionId \u2192 {start, end}) that overrides prop-driven positions while a resize is active. During pointermove, update this map AND re-render the affected action at its new width. On pointerup, clear the override and let the committed data flow back through props. If you skip this, clips will appear frozen during resize and then jump to their final position.\n\n**Scroll sync (second most likely regression):** The current layout has track labels in a fixed-width (144px) left sidebar and the Timeline in a flex-1 right pane. These are sibling containers. The library's internal scroll handled vertical alignment implicitly because track labels were outside its scope \u2014 BUT the track-label container has its own vertical overflow. You need to wire an onScroll handler that keeps the track-label pane's scrollTop in sync with the grid's scrollTop (or vice versa). Test this with enough tracks to require scrolling.\n\n**onChange removal safety:** Verify by reading useClipEditing.ts carefully. The onChange handler calls `applyTimelineEdit(nextRows, buildRowTrackPatches(nextRows))`. During library-managed resize, this was the path that updated row data. With custom resize, useClipResize.onActionResizeEnd calls its own applyTimelineEdit. Double-check there's no other code path that triggers onChange.\n\n**DOM query migration (T5) must be done before or simultaneously with T6.** If you swap in TimelineCanvas while drop-position.ts still queries `.timeline-editor-edit-area`, all drops will silently fail or miscalculate. Consider adding the `.timeline-canvas-edit-area` class to the grid container in T3 so T5 can be validated independently.\n\n**pixelsPerSecond calculation:** In the library, `pixelsPerSecond = scaleWidth / scale`. Verify this formula is correct by checking coordinate-utils or the library source. All action positioning depends on this being right.\n\n**Testing approach for T9:** Don't just check that things work \u2014 check that positions are CORRECT. Create a clip, note its from/to times, verify the clip's pixel position matches `time * pixelsPerSecond + startLeft`. Resize a clip and verify the metadata update matches the visual. This catches off-by-one-startLeft errors.\n\n**Single PR:** User wants one PR, all in one go. But develop incrementally \u2014 you can render TimelineCanvas alongside Timeline temporarily during T2-T3 for visual comparison before the swap in T6."

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app

        Idea:
Replace @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor entirely with a custom lightweight timeline renderer. The library bundles interact.js (~85KB minified) and we only use it for: time ruler rendering, row layout with virtualization, resize via interact.js, and cursor/playhead rendering. We've already reimplemented drag (useClipDrag), drop (useExternalDrop, useDragCoordinator), and cross-track moves ourselves. The goal is to eliminate the ~250KB minified dependency entirely and build a custom <TimelineCanvas> using our existing hooks + a simple pointer-event resize handler. Key files: src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineEditor.tsx (main consumer), useClipDrag.ts, useClipResize.ts, useDragCoordinator.ts, useExternalDrop.ts, coordinate-utils.ts. The library provides: Timeline component from @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor, TimelineAction/TimelineRow/TimelineState types from @xzdarcy/timeline-engine, CSS from @xzdarcy/react-timeline-editor/dist/react-timeline-editor.css.

User notes and answers:
- User answers: Q1: Clean reimplementation acceptable, not pixel-perfect parity. Q2: Might have 50+ tracks, so consider virtualization. Q3: Simple pointer-event resize with min-width clamping is sufficient, no inertia/snap needed. Q4: One PR, all in one go.

    "id": "T4",
    "description": "Add pointer-event resize handles to each action wrapper in `<TimelineCanvas>`. Left and right thin (8px) absolute-positioned divs with `cursor: ew-resize`. On pointerdown: capture pointer, record initial start/end and clientX, fire `onActionResizeStart({action, row, dir})`. On pointermove: compute new start or end from delta, clamp >= 0 and enforce minimum width, update TRANSIENT local action geometry in TimelineCanvas state so the clip visually resizes during drag, and fire `onActionResizing({action, row, dir})`. On pointerup: fire `onActionResizeEnd({action, row, dir})` and clear transient state. CRITICAL: TimelineCanvas must own a transient override map (e.g. `resizingOverrides: Map<actionId, {start, end}>`) that takes precedence over prop-driven positions during active resize. Without this, clips will snap rather than smoothly resize. The callback signatures must match what `useClipResize.ts` expects so it requires zero changes.",

Pointerdown captures the handle pointer, fires `onActionResizeStart({ action, row, dir })`, pointermove updates the transient geometry and fires `onActionResizing({ action, row, start, end, dir })`, and pointerup fires `onActionResizeEnd({ action, row, start, end, dir })` before clearing the override.   "meta_commentary": "EXECUTION GUIDANCE:\n\n**The two hardest parts of this plan are T3 (grid shell) and T4 (resize handles). Get these right and everything else follows.**\n\n**Resize transient state (most likely source of bugs):** The library previously owned action geometry and updated it internally during resize drags. Our useClipResize hook only records start position on resize-start and commits the final edit on resize-end \u2014 it does NOT drive visual updates during the drag. TimelineCanvas must maintain a `resizingOverrides` map (actionId \u2192 {start, end}) that overrides prop-driven positions while a resize is active. During pointermove, update this map AND re-render the affected action at its new width. On pointerup, clear the override and let the committed data flow back through props. If you skip this, clips will appear frozen during resize and then jump to their final position.\n\n**Scroll sync (second most likely regression):** The current layout has track labels in a fixed-width (144px) left sidebar and the Timeline in a flex-1 right pane. These are sibling containers. The library's internal scroll handled vertical alignment implicitly because track labels were outside its scope \u2014 BUT the track-label container has its own vertical overflow. You need to wire an onScroll handler that keeps the track-label pane's scrollTop in sync with the grid's scrollTop (or vice versa). Test this with enough tracks to require scrolling.\n\n**onChange removal safety:** Verify by reading useClipEditing.ts carefully. The onChange handler calls `applyTimelineEdit(nextRows, buildRowTrackPatches(nextRows))`. During library-managed resize, this was the path that updated row data. With custom resize, useClipResize.onActionResizeEnd calls its own applyTimelineEdit. Double-check there's no other code path that triggers onChange.\n\n**DOM query migration (T5) must be done before or simultaneously with T6.** If you swap in TimelineCanvas while drop-position.ts still queries `.timeline-editor-edit-area`, all drops will silently fail or miscalculate. Consider adding the `.timeline-canvas-edit-area` class to the grid container in T3 so T5 can be validated independently.\n\n**pixelsPerSecond calculation:** In the library, `pixelsPerSecond = scaleWidth / scale`. Verify this formula is correct by checking coordinate-utils or the library source. All action positioning depends on this being right.\n\n**Testing approach for T9:** Don't just check that things work \u2014 check that positions are CORRECT.
